#2934. Knicks' Mikal Bridges drills buzzer-beating 3-pointer to defeat Trail Blazers

The New York Knicks are rounding into form, and maybe Saturday night is what Mikal Bridges needed to get back on track, too.

The 30-year-old wing, who came into a matchup with the Portland Trail Blazers shooting just north of 40% from the field and a smidge above 30% from deep, drilled a buzzer-beating 3-pointer to deliver New York a 97-96 victory in Madison Square Garden.

Bridges finished with 12 points on 5-of-8 shooting. He connected on two of his three attempts from long range. The last one extended the Knicks' win streak to four games.

Following a pair of Deni Avdija free throws that put the Trail Blazers (10-6) up 96-94, New York (10-6) took a timeout. With 3.2 seconds remaining, Josh Hart inbounded the ball to Bridges.

He caught the pass to the right of the top of the arc, used a quick fake to get an overzealous Toumani Camara to bite, took one more dribble and launched.

The ball rattled in, the buzzer sounded and Mike Breen exclaimed, "Bang! Bang!"

Bridges was swarmed by his Knicks teammates, eventually wearing a smile as MSG reveled in utter pandemonium.

He came to the rescue on a night when New York squandered a 26-point lead. The Knicks shot 13 of 27 from 3-point land in the first half but scored only 31 points over the final two quarters.

Bridges admitted afterward that Portland played "harder" than New York in the second half.

But he also noted in that on-court postgame interview: "It's 48 minutes. We just kept persevering through the time and ended up winning the game."

After trailing 94-84 with less than five minutes to go, the Trail Blazers went on a 12-0 run, which culminated in the aforementioned Avdija free throws. Additionally, that stretch featured a game-tying 3-pointer from Damian Lillard.

Avdija was sent to the line because of a head-scratching foul by Knicks center Andre Drummond, who switched and committed the personal on the floor well beyond the arc.

Avdija had 20 points. Center Donovan Clingan spearheaded Portland's effort with 22 points and 13 rebounds. Lillard put up just 15 points and shot a meager 1 of 9 from outside.

New York was paced by Jalen Brunson's 30 points. Fellow guard Landry Shamet poured in 20 points.

Bridges, though, was the hero, sinking a redemptive shot 16 games into what's been a trying season for the ninth-year pro.

#2929. Mikal Bridges’ buzzer-beater saves Knicks from brutal choke

For most of this season, Mikal Bridges had been benched in these moments.

With the Knicks missing Karl-Anthony Towns (illness), OG Anunoby (left calf tightness) and Jordan Clarkson (right plantar fasciitis) on Saturday, Bridges found himself on the court in the closing moments, and the ball ended up in his hands with the game on the line.

Mikal Bridges hits the game-winning, buzzer-beating 3-pointer in the Knicks' 97-96 win over the Trail Blazers on Nov. 21, 2026, at Madison Square Garden. Getty Images

He promptly delivered what just might be his coming out party for this season, drilling a buzzer-beating 3-pointer as the shorthanded Knicks staved off the Trail Blazers 97-96 at Madison Square Garden to extend their winning streak to four games.

The Knicks saw a 23-point halftime lead disappear.

After Deni Avdija's free throws broke a tie to give the Blazers a two-point lead with 3.2 seconds left, the Knicks needed a hero.

A leaping Josh Hart celebrates with Mikal Bridges as Landry Shamet (left) looks to join in on the celebration after Bridges' buzzer-beating 3-pointer in the Knicks' win over the Trail Blazers at the Garden. Jason Szenes for New York Post

Bridges — amid a season of intense criticism and struggles — became one.

The play was, as expected, supposed to be for Jalen Brunson. But he was covered. So Bridges instead got the inbounds from Josh Hart, pump-faked Toumani Camara out of the way and drilled the game-winning 25-footer from the left wing.

"Knowing that any good and bad stretch in the NBA can change very quickly," Landry Shamet, one of Bridges' closest friends on the team, told The Post, "he's always in a position to put himself in a good situation."

With the game tied at 94-94 and the Knicks in the penalty, Andre Drummond committed a boneheaded foul on Avdija, who was dribbling in the opposite direction of the basket near halfcourt. Drummond later admitted he thought the Knicks had a foul to give.

Avdija's free throws looked like they would seal what would have been the Knicks' worst loss of the season.

Before long, though, the Garden was erupting, and Bridges' teammates surrounded him in celebration.

Jalen Brunson, who scored 30 points, celebrates after hitting a 3-pointer in the Knicks' buzzer-beating win over the Trail Blazers at the Garden. Jason Szenes for New York Post

"Shoutout to Mikal for making the big-time 3," Drummond said, "to kind of mask the nonsense that happened."

Nobody has come under more scrutiny than Bridges this season, and nobody needed a win more.

"That's just who he is," Brunson said. "I don't know how many times I've said this before, but when you continuously work on your game every single day, and things may not be falling in your favor, but you keep doing the right thing every single day, you keep chipping away, chipping away, eventually things are gonna turn for you. And over the past week or two, things have been turning for him."

Landry Shamet, who was in the starting lineup and scored 20 points, grabs a rebound during the first half of the Knicks' buzzer-beating win over the Trail Blazers at the Garden. Jason Szenes for New York Post

It was Bridges' second game-winner against the Blazers. He did it two years ago as well.

"I like the city of Portland," Bridges joked.

The Knicks are making a habit of seeing big leads dissipate. It hasn't come back to bite them yet, but they are treading in dangerous waters. Saturday's letup came on the heels of watching a 16-point fourth-quarter lead cut down to three points in the final moments of their win over the Timberwolves on Thursday.

Andre Drummond, who started in place of Karl-Anthony Towns (illness), looks to block Jayson Kent's layup attempt during the Knicks' buzzer-beating win over the Trail Blazers at the Garden. Jason Szenes for New York Post

They drilled their first eight 3-pointers and erupted for 40 points in the first quarter to jump out to a 17-point lead. Brunson, who made his first five 3-pointers, scored 19 points in that period and 23 by halftime. Shamet, continuing his red-hot start to the season, followed suit with 14 first-half points. The Knicks shot 53.2 percent from the field and 48.1 percent from deep in the first half and took a 23-point lead into the break.

They held Portland to a brutal 34.9 percent shooting from the field and 21.7 percent shooting from behind the arc in that first half.

Considering the quality of opponent and who the Knicks were missing, it was two of the best quarters the Knicks have played this season.

But the Knicks' red-hot shooting suddenly went ice-cold in the second half. They were outscored 29-15 in the third quarter and 24-16 in the fourth.

Their 31 second-half points were a dramatic drop-off from the 66 they erupted for in the first half. It was a complete role reversal, as the Blazers caught fire offensively — until Bridges bailed them out.

"He's been there and done that," head coach Mike Brown said. "So for him to have the ball in his hands at that point in the game, there's a lot of confidence not only coming from him but from his teammates and this staff."

The three absences meant a trio of usually end-of-bench reserves saw action. James Wiseman, Pacôme Dadiet and Dalton Knecht — who was called up from the G-League on Saturday — all featured in non-garbage-time minutes.

Dadiet actually was back on the floor to start the fourth quarter. In total, 11 Knicks saw the court — Mohamed Diawara was the only active player not to enter the game.

It meant a pretty uneven scoring output. Brunson and Shamet combined for 50, more than half of the team's points.

But it was Bridges who provided the most important ones.

#2922. San Antonio vs. New Orleans, Final Score: Wembanyama’s 50 points leads Spurs to 114-91 win

Victor Wembanyama blew up on the Pelicans with a 32 point first half showing and 50 overall, and while San Antonio (14-3) allowed New Orleans (5-13) make two runs in the first half, they were not troubled after the first half in winning 114-91. The Spurs outscored the visitors in the first three quarters, and Wembanyama and Stephon Castle slammed the door shut to get the starters some early rest before their brief West Coast road trip. Wembanyama (50 points, 19 rebounds, 5 blocks, and 4 steals) put up the types of numbers with this transcendent performance that might be used to make the case for him in the MVP battle with Luka Doncic.

Wembanyama (31 points on 12-for-15 FG, 13 rebounds, and 3 blocks) had the type of first half that so many bigs would kill for in a whole game. Stephon Castle (19 points and 6 rebounds) found his outside shooting touch with five threes. De'Aaron Fox dished out 10 assists.

Jeremiah Fears (29 points, 5 rebounds, 4 assists, and 2 steals) fearlessly kept the Pelicans in striking distance for 2+ periods. Jordan Poole (25 points) shot the ball well to support his backcourt mate as the pair made more than half of their team's field goals and points for the evening. Second year big Derik Queen chipped in 15 points and 15 rebounds.

With Zion Williamson (eye) being a late scratch minutes before tipoff, New Orleans looked out of sorts from the get-go. When the Spurs coasted their way to a 15-4 lead, Fears decided to take things into his own hands to nearly single-handedly erase that deficit in minutes. Poole came into the game and put up nine points frighteningly fast, and in a couple of blinks, the Pelicans were up three. Jordan McLaughlin helped preserve a disastrous quarter with hustle and grit, and San Antonio appeared lucky to be up 32-29.

Wembanyama asserted himself forcefully well at the start of the second period, as the Spurs went on a 13-2 run. His scores seemingly came from all over the court throuhgout the first half. The middle of the period run happened again as New Orleans threatened San Antonio with a 9-1 sequence. Fox, in particular, while kept out of the scorebook, was a noticeable menace on defense in guarding Fears. Wembanyama was the Spurs' fulcrum on offense (32 of the Spurs' 61) to help his team get out of the half up 16.

After Wembanyama's inevitable slowdown in the third period, Castle made the most of his diet of outside shots – with his four three-pointers putting New Orleans back on their heels and supplied San Antonio with the necessary cushion to secure its 14th win of the season.

I do like this version of Fox that's more pesky on both ends – he ran down Vassell's first miss, which ultimately led to a corner three and then he harangued Fears to cause a turnover on the ensuing inbounds. Fox, later in the half, bothered Fears into a backcourt violation and was whistled for a technical as the two had to be separated.

Though I'm not a native Spanish speaker, I thought it very humourous that a local mariachi band performed a song with "llorar" in its refrain after San Antonio ran its lead to 20.

Has there been a bigger dropoff from someone who was critical to his team's chances in an NBA Finals (2022) to wasting away in obscurity (2026) as Poole? There's also probably a similar, if not more statement to be made for Dejounte Murray… and well, Williamson whenever he changes teams.

The team that introduced the corner three as a useful offensive concept in the 2000s missed quite a bit of them this evening with an alarming amount of them airballs.

San Antonio and Memphis are the only undefeated teams left on the road(?!)

Fox-Slamma-Jamma-Wembanyama #1: While New Orleans pelted the rim and backboard with some unsightly attempts, Fox found his center with a lob partway through the opening stanza to push the Spurs lead to 11.

Carter Bryant really did seem confined to the Bruce Bowen corners for his offense in the first three quarters. He did connect on two threes later in the second half.

Sequence of the Game #1: Late in a wildly swinging first quarter, McLaughlin – giving 12 inches away to Karlo Matkovic, swiped the ball away from the big man, kicked it upcourt to Fox, who found Wembanyama for a non-shunk lob.

Sequence of the Game #2: Late in the third quarter, Wembanyama swatted away a Missi dunk attempt, and then drew an offensive foul call on Pierre, Jr. to cause the turnover.

Sequence of the Game #3: After Bryant missed a three in the fourth quarter, the long carom fell into Harrison Barnes' hands, and the second year guard cut baseline, received Barnes' pass, and threw down a thunderous two-hand slam.

Tarris Reed, Jr. (lithe AND bruising big man) playing time: In contesting a Poole floater, the rookie accidentally kneed the Pelican in the torso. He did get to four rebounds (two offensive) in his six minutes of playing tie.

Vassell bricked his first three attempts, but Wembanyama tip-slammed home the third try. Wembanyama, while passing it to Vassell, ended up smacking Queen right in the face, which took the Pelican momentarily out of the action. Two Champagnie triples put the Spurs up six. The Pelicans halfcourt offense during the initial minutes was really difficult to describe – Yves Missi managed to overshoot the rim by a good 5+ feet on a baseline jumper and many other possessions ended in long jumpers. Vassell willed in his fourth three attempt, and the San Antonio lead swelled to 11. Fears, like a robot that suddenly came to life, went on a 9-point burst, and with Poole's trio of threes, the Pelicans erased the deficit because… killer-instinct-still-in-process. Wembanyama then went on his own 9-point eruption to end the quarter with San Antonio up three.

San Antonio methodically started building out its lead to start the second. Then, on a foul committed on Wembanyama, someone named Jaron Pierre, Jr. shoved him after the play, and the rookie and Keldon Johnson were assessed double technicals. (It was cool to hear the referee refer to the superstar as 'Wemby' after the decision came back from Secaucus). The free throws pushed Wembanyama's point total past 20 a mere 14+ minutes into game action. The center's corner three then put the Pelicans down 12. However, right around the same time as it occurred in the previous period, New Orleans burned hot while the Spurs went cold, and a 14-point game became seven – with a Vassell free throw was the only Spurs damage during the Pelicans' 9-1 push. Then, just like in the first quarter, Wembanyama ran off a series of buckets to get himself over the 30-point mark, and San Antonio coasted into halftime up 61-45.

Normally one of New Orleans' biggest contributors, Bey's first and only points came two minutes into the third quarter. Castle, on a pair of catch-and-shoot threes, helped get the Spurs back up past 20. After San Antonio made it 69-48, Fears tried to kickstart yet another comeback attempt, but Castle countered with his third and fourth triples of the period preventing the Pelicans from trimming the deficit. Bryant snagged a pair of rebounds in traffic despite no offensive success to that point. Johnson and Fox took turns finding their superstar center deep in the paint for lay-up on successive possessions, and then teamed with each other for a quarter-ending Johnson three. The Spurs went to the fourth up 89-66.

For the Pelicans fan's perspective, please visit Pelican Debrief.

San Antonio returns to Northern California to take on Darius Acuff and the Sacramento Kings Monday night at 9:00 PM CDT.

#2921. Travis Kelce continues to make history while helping the Chiefs win

The Kansas City Chiefs' offense continued its tear through the middle of the schedule with another 30-plus-point performance, their sixth of the season, against the Arizona Cardinals in Week 11.

The Chiefs got off to a hot start, and so did tight end Travis Kelce, who caught two touchdowns in the first half. He finished with a team-high seven targets and six catches for 74 yards.

They were the 109th and 110th scores of Kelce's career, both in his 100th game at Arrowhead Stadium (he became the fourth Chiefs player ever to do so). The touchdowns pushed him up the list with the all-time greats, surpassing former New England Patriots legend Rob Gronkowski (108) to rank third in history, behind Tony Gonzalez (115) and Antonio Gates (118).

Kelce's fifth touchdown of the season matched or exceeded his year-long totals from each of the last three years

Kelce also became the second-oldest tight end ever to score two touchdowns in a game (since 1970), less than 200 days younger than Gonzalez when he topped the list in 2013.

He was also just the fourth player in NFL history with two or more scrimmage touchdowns in a half at age 37 or older, according to the CBS broadcast. He joined Jerry Rice, Steve Young and Jim Thorpe.

#2919. Tokenization platform Securitize gains 10% postmarket on Q3 results, move into asset-backed finance

Tokenization platform Securitize climbed about 10% postmarket after reporting third quarter growth alongside increased operating expenses, and announcing an expansion into asset-backed finance through a strategic acquisition.

Miami-based Securitize, which made its public market debut in July, operates in the relatively new corner of the crypto equity universe involved in institutional tokenization infrastructure. Tokenized assets on the platform grew 9% in the third quarter, to $4.8 billion, from the same period a year ago. Aggregate transaction volume rose 20% from a year ago, to $4.3 billion. Revenue of $11.5 million was up 28% from last year.

The growth came as bullish sentiment around tokenization gained traction throughout the third quarter, ahead of the Security and Exchange Commission formalizing the long awaited Innovation Exemption on Sept. 17, which created a temporary pathway for trading of certain tokenized stocks and strengthened the regulatory outlook.

Profitability is still meaningfully below year-ago levels, due in part to costs associated with going public over the summer, as well as the scaling up of the tokenization business at an active time for the entire sector, Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

Securitize reinforced "certain areas of the company like finance, compliance, legal [and] cybersecurity," which increased corporate costs "compared to a year ago when we were privately held," Domingo said. "We're in in a very good position. The adjusted EBITDA … is improving significantly from previous quarters."

Securitize reported an adjusted EBITDA loss of $2 million, compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $3.6 million a year ago, better than a $5.5 million loss in the second quarter. The company posted a 183% increase in operating costs and expenses.

Also on Monday, the Securitize board authorized a $50 million share buyback and announced the acquisition of River One, an institutional asset-backed financing platform developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets. Terms were not disclosed.

Securitize sees an opportunity to modernize the $9 trillion market for asset backed finance by using stablecoins to finance real world assets, generate yield and tokenize investment positions and credit products, Domingo said.The acquisition also marks an expansion beyond Securitize's core niche markets at a time when tokenization's competitive landscape is growing, with traditional asset managers like BlackRock and Franklin Templeton, and established financial market infrastructure providers like DTCC and Broadridge, entering the industry.

"We have been very active in tokenized funds, treasuries [and] yield … And we've been obviously recently very active in tokenized stocks, so this adds another piece of the puzzle in the emerging tokenization space for us to capture revenue growth."

#2916. Victor Wembanyama says he was showing off for Selena Gomez while scoring 50 points in win over Pelicans

Victor Wembanyama outscored the New Orleans Pelicans' starters in the first half on Saturday night at home. He had 32 points. They had 26.

By game's end, Wembanyama had scored 50 points, his second 50-point performance of the season, this time in a 114-91 victory over the Pelicans (5-13) at Frost Bank Center.

The 7-foot-4 superstar went for 52 points in a Nov. 4 win over the Dallas Mavericks. Both of those outings have coincided with San Antonio (14-3) going 14-1 following an 0-2 start.

Wembanyama, still only 22, has already tied Spurs legend David Robinson for the second-most 50-plus-point games in franchise history with three. George Gervin, a Basketball Hall of Famer like Robinson, leads the way with five.

Wembanyama's got a long runway to surpass both Robinson and Gervin in that department. A third 50-piece this season would be historic in itself.

He's the third player since 1973-74 to log at least 50 points, 15 rebounds and 5 blocks in a single game. Wembanyama clocked out after shooting 20 of 29 with 18 rebounds, 5 blocks and 4 steals.

"It is very special," Wembanyama said, when asked about reaching the 50-point threshold in an on-court postgame interview.

He then said with a smile: "But unlike many of my peers, I'm not very good at stat padding. So I haven't reached it as many times as I should have."

Actress Selena Gomez was sitting courtside at the game. Wembanyama noticed.

"Of course, I had to show off for Selena, you know," he said, laughing.

The Spurs pulled away in the second quarter. San Antonio also got 19 points from guard Stephon Castle. Another backcourt contributor, De'Aaron Fox, piled up 10 assists.

The Pelicans are 1-9 in their last 10 games. They've come undone after beginning the 2026-27 campaign with a 4-4 record. A bright spot for New Orleans is the continued growth of 20-year-old guard Jeremiah Fears, who led the team with 29 points in the loss.

As for Wembanyama, he's once again an NBA MVP candidate. It's looking like he'll face stiff competition for that award this season as well.

Los Angeles Lakers star Luka Dončić has a pair of 50-point games under his belt, too, and he's leading the league in scoring with 37.2 points per contest.

#2912. Wembanyama’s 50-bomb demolishes the Pelicans

It is far too early to talk about the NBA's Most Valuable Player award. But Victor Wembanyama is making it clear that he belongs in the conversation – whenever it's time to have it.

Wembanyama had another dominant performance in the San Antonio Spurs' 114-91 win over the New Orleans Pelicans. San Antonio's big man had 50 points, 19 rebounds, 4 steals, and 5 blocks.

Wembanyama was red-hot from three. He knocked down five of his eleven three-pointers, taking advantage of the Pelicans' relaxed defense. It wasn't just their perimeter defense that struggled; Wembanyama was able to get open for lobs all night. He shot 20-for-29 from the field, getting a lot of good looks inside the arc.

Wembanyama's low post game has been one of the most interesting developments in his game this season. He looks legitimately more comfortable with his back to the basket. His baseline fadeaway has been money, and his touch on turnaround hook shots is soft. I'm a sucker for a good up-and-under move, but Wembanyama doesn't normally utilize them. This was a sweet one, tricking Yves Missi and leading to the dunk.

Wembanyama wasn't the only high-flying star for the Spurs. Stephon Castle had 19 points and 6 assists on an efficient 7-for-12 shooting. Castle has already recorded some of the NBA's highest vertical leaps on dunks. This transition lob has to be close to the peak of his jumping ability.

Castle was on fire from deep, hitting five of his seven three-pointers. The Spurs desperately need him to hit open shots. When he does that, the offense sings. The blowout win over the Pelicans is an example of what is possible when he's hitting threes.

This is a great example of what defenses are doing to the Spurs, and why Castle is open so often. Jeremiah Fears is tagging the roller, leaving Castle wide open because he's not a knockdown threat from deep. This blows up the pick-and-roll, but leaves Castle free on the lift. He hits the shot, making New Orleans pay for leaving him open.

Carter Bryant recorded one of the most powerful dunks of the game. There have to be more ways for him to utilize his athletic finishing ability inside without driving to the basket. He's not a strong ball-handler, but could he become a top-of-the-line cutter and offensive rebounder so he can get more attempts around the basket, rather than being a spacer off the ball? Figuring that out could take Bryant's game to the next level. The sophomore had one of his better games of the season with 8 points, hitting two of his threes.

#2908. Why Anthropic's blockbuster IPO couldn't escape the stock market correction

No one can say the Anthropic initial public offering wasn't a success for the company. Deal size and pricing were signs of what IPO experts say was insatiable demand for "ANT" shares. But what came within a day is something that investors better get used to: anxiety in the stock market about valuations being assigned to artificial intelligence revenue and business models that should keep Anthropic shares volatile in the period ahead.

The Anthropic IPO reinforces those fears rather than running counter to them, says Avery Marquez, director of investment strategies at Renaissance Capital, which researches IPOs and manages the Renaissance IPO ETF (IPO).

"The volatility in the AI trade we've been seeing for the past few months stems from valuations and whether we are in a bubble, and that's been heightened and highlighted by the Anthropic IPO," Marquez said on "ETF Edge" on Friday.

And in the case of Anthropic, which finished trading Friday down 9%, the valuation anxiety becomes "so much more prevalent," she added, because it is a new stock.

It is not just any new stock, but one that has Wall Street adding downside risks to research notes likely never seen before, and which may be the greatest understatement (or most subtle equities research joke) in the history of earnings analysis. "Anthropic could develop AI systems which become fully autonomous and wipe out humanity which may lead to a serious decline in earnings," Seaport Research Partners analyst wrote Jay Goldberg wrote on Nov. 18.

"It was challenging company to value ... and it's very richly valued," Marquez said. "We're seeing something more reasonable and investors grappling now with where should this company actually be valued," she added.

The fact that Anthropic pulled off the deal in this market and was able to command the price it did was an achievement, according to Yuri Khodjamirian, chief investment officer at Tema ETFs, which offers multiple funds that invest in pre-IPO shares of companies and held Anthropic in its Tema Photonics & Optical ETF (LAZR) well before IPO day. "We have to remember they pulled off a very large IPO in the middle of a very severe market with the volatility in the tech sector specifically," Khodjamirian said on "ETF Edge."

Khodjamirian says the investor demand for AI remains, but amid a reassessment of returns. And he says an equally hard task has now begun for Anthropic. Investors are unwinding positions in everything from tech stocks to gold and bitcoin, and repositioning for 2027 based on their new outlooks. "We are in a bit of vacuum," he said.

"From here on the market is trying trying to figure out what direction to go in," Khodjamirian said, whether that is based on Fed policy, geopolitics, the midterm elections results, or the AI trade. "In many cases, the chief investment officers I speak to, and the clients, say 'I'm just gonna take some risk off and wait and see until I get more info.' And with the Anthropic, the revenue picture and how the AI adoption curve progresses will be a vital stat to watch," he added.

Plenty of value has already been wiped out in tech, according to Khodjamirian, but investors need new signals to buy. That doesn't just go for Anthropic but for much of the market's tech leadership. "One of the things most difficult for investors to understand right now is some of the leadership in the market that has been taken by semiconductor companies," he said, adding that the peak was 22% of S&P 500 earnings. But these companies, like Nvidia and Micron, have a very different earnings profile from the rest of the market.

The VanEck Semiconductor ETF (SMH), which was down over 10% on Friday and around 15% for the week, was on pace for its worst week since September 2001, according to CNBC data on Friday afternoon. It also lost 15% in one week of March 2020. The tech sector of the S&P 500, tracked by the State Street Technology Select Sector SPDR ETF (XLK), turned in its worst weekly performance since the Covid crash.

Khodjamirian said while these stocks may look cheap on a P/E basis right now after the heavy selling, that requires faith in the earnings estimates, which he says will be more important as results are released for the next few years. "Right now it looks cheap, but will companies like Micron and Nvidia actually deliver?"

Nvidia shares fell 14% on Friday, adding to its worst stretch of weekly performance since 2008. What's more, the single biggest trade in the options market on Friday was a near $100-million bet that the VanEck Semiconductor ETF has a long way more to fall.

And the same caution about the outlook goes for Anthropic, he said. "That's why we need to watch Anthropic's annual recurring revenue number, because it is a key part of puzzle," he said.

Marquez said there is always hype in names like SpaceX and Anthropic when they go public, and it is hard to know whether the pricing of the deal is based on fundamentals, "but we just need more time and lots price discovery," she said. "It is not going to happen overnight," she added.

As an IPO researcher, she says this "discernment" is a healthy thing for the markets. There are few cases to look to as comps for Anthropic, other than SpaceX, and before that, way back to Facebook's IPO in 2012. And neither example is one of quick recovery. SpaceX shares remain well below their first-day closing price more than five months after its IPO. Facebook (now Meta), meanwhile, "did not do well for the first year after going public and we see where it is now. It's just the nature of these disruptive tech companies that come to market," Marquez said. "They lend themselves to more volatility and a longer period of price discovery for investors."

All eyes will next be on Anthropic's rival OpenAI, which has suggested it will be ready to go public in 2027. Marquez said the reaction to the Anthropic deal may be a signal that it was "a good idea to wait, allowing themselves to build more quarters of better financials and improve fundamentals a bit before taking on a market that is really quite discerning."

Tema does not own shares of OpenAI in any of its ETFs. But, "I can't say it wont be," Khodjamirian said. "They exist out there in the secondary market, and we will look at it, but we haven't made any decision. It depends on the ETF fit and if the portfolio manager wants to invest. We are always looking out for these interesting businesses but never just for the sake of private exposure," he added.

Tema does own Databricks, Stripe, Kalshi and Polymarket across its funds. It also was an investor in SpaceX pre-IPO through its Tema Space Innovators ETF (NASA).

Khodjamirian said demand from public market investors to access these "generational businesses" will continue to be the case, despite the market volatility, and will more often come before IPO day. In fact, he pointed to the filing from the world's largest asset manager and one of the biggest ETF companies in the world, BlackRock, earlier this week. It wants to bring out a new kind of exchange-traded product that would bring the ETF and interval fund structures together as a way to increase exposure to private companies, with shares trading on an exchange while the fund makes monthly repurchase offers.

#2907. Mikal Bridges buzzer-beater, Victor Wembanyama monster stats highlight NBA action

There were six NBA games on Saturday, Nov. 21, which is a lighter schedule for the 30-team league.

But fewer games did not mean a shortage of action. Stars across the country had impressive nights, from career-highs to a thrilling buzzer-beater.

Victor Wembanyama had a monster night by scoring 50 points as the anchor of an incredibly rare statline to keep the San Antonio Spurs' momentum strong.

Tyler Herro also scored 50 points, which was the Milwaukee Bucks star's career-high.

Elsewhere, Mikal Bridges wowed with a buzzer-beater to give the New York Knicks their fourth straight win.

Here's a breakdown of the NBA highlights from the day:

Wembanyama proved why his nickname is the Alien in the Spurs' 114-91 win over the New Orleans Pelicans. The San Antonio big man posted 50 points, 19 rebounds, four steals and five blocks in the effort.

According to the NBA, Wembanyama is the first player since the 1973-74 season to notch 50 points, at least 15 boards, four steals and five blocks in one game. ESPN adds that he is the first player in league history to have 50 points, five blocks and five made 3-point shots in a single matchup.

He is also the third San Antonio player to record multiple 50-point games in the same season. He had 52 points and 24 rebounds earlier this month in a win over the Dallas Mavericks.

The Spurs have won 14 of their last 15 games and had a 12-game winning streak that was broken earlier in the week.

The national hype might be around Herro's former team, the Miami Heat, with the release of "Grand Theft Auto 6." But the Bucks guard made basketball fans pay attention to him when he scored a career-high 50 points. He added 11 rebounds and almost scored the game-tying 3-point bucket, but his shot was milliseconds too late and the Bucks lost to LeBron James and the Philadelphia 76ers 132-129.

According to the Bucks, Herro, who joined the Heat in the Giannis Antetokounmpo trade, is the first player in NBA history to have at least 50 points, 11 boards, seven 3-point shots, and shoot 65% from the field in a single game.

Joel Embiid led the 76ers with 26 points, 17 rebounds and nine assists.

Mikal Bridges wowed by scoring the game-winning shot for the Knicks at the buzzer.

With 3.2 seconds left, he took the inbound from Josh Hart, dribbled and took a side step in front of Portland Trail Blazers forward Toumani Camara and then launched a shot from behind the arc that drained the net smoothly.

The Knicks beat the Trail Blazers 97-96 after Portland came back from a 26-point deficit. Bridges finished with 12 points and Finals MVP Jalen Brunson led New York with 30 points and seven rebounds.

#2905. What's ahead for AI financing after Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang departs

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's departure hasn't produced a broad shock to the credit market, but it is putting the company's role as a financier of artificial intelligence infrastructure under scrutiny.

Huang, who stepped down Thursday amid an espionage and adultery scandal, built Nvidia into an AI powerhouse in the 33 years since he co-founded the company. Chief financial officer Colette Kress has been named interim CEO as the company searches for a permanent chief executive.

Nvidia not only supplies its graphics processing units to hyperscalers and tech giants, it also provides financing to other companies to help the industry expand. That could all change.

"Although we expect the company to fulfill all of its previously announced commitments, we believe that going forward, it may be more difficult for the company to negotiate new multi-party investments among ecosystem partners at the same scale it has done up to this point," Goldman Sachs analyst James Schneider said in a note Thursday.

The news of Huang's resignation comes after investors were already concerned about the massive amount of debt being taken on to fund AI infrastructure.

Tech bond yields have risen and spreads have widened on credit default swaps, most notably in lower-rated names. Credit default swaps are contracts in which investors pay for protection in case the borrower can't repay its debt. Wider spreads indicate investors view the bonds as riskier.

On Friday, the yield on Nvidia's 4.5% bond due in 2031 sat at 5.717%, nearly 5% higher week to date. Bond yields move inversely to prices.

Tech stocks slumped Friday with the Nasdaq 100 closing down 1.2% as markets continued to digest the Nvidia news. Yields on hyperscaler bonds ticked slightly higher on Friday, signaling the broader concern about AI debt lingers.

It's the smaller AI companies that could ultimately see an impact if Nvidia makes any changes to its AI financing. The company had $36 billion in off-balance-sheet AI cloud agreements, or backstops, for neoclouds, according to its last quarterly financial filing in July.

Neoclouds CoreWeave, Nebius and IREN are all in Nvidia's portfolio. Their shares were down sharply Thursday.

CoreWeave, which has become the poster child for AI debt fears, saw its bond yields retreat after its quarterly earnings on Tuesday eased investor concerns. The yield on its 9.75% bond due in 2031 was 14.79% on Friday after climbing to 16.7% last week. The spread on its five-year CDS also dropped more than 17% week to date, although it remained above 1,000 — which is generally considered a sign of distress.

It remains unclear how Nvidia's support for those companies may change, said CreditSights analyst Andy Li.

"We don't know what the ultimate strategy for Nvidia is going to be coming out the other side," he said.

"However, I think directionally, just given the fact that Jensen was so crucial in selling the Nvidia story and being a very aggressive deal maker, it's likely that a lot of this creative deal-making and circular financing and equity and credit support is going to moderate," he added.

CoreWeave CEO Michael Intrator told CNBC Wednesday, before Huang's departure, that Nvidia "is not one person."

"There are thousands of people over there that interface with thousands of people at my company every day. They integrate on the technology. They integrate on the physical infrastructure. They integrate on the networking," he said in an interview with "Squawk on the Street." "They have been a great partner for us for years. They will continue to be a great partner for us."

Bob Lang, founder and chief strategist at Explosive Options, doesn't see any big changes in terms of Nvidia's relevance or its ability to finance for now. As CFO, Kress was involved in the financing deals, and the search for a permanent CEO is going to take some time, he noted.

The real issue is just how much money the company will want to lend for AI financing, rather than, say using its hundreds of billions in free cash flow for other purposes, like share buybacks, capex, or acquisitions.

"Big companies like Nvidia and Meta, they're holding all the cards," he said. "When they decide that the game is over, that's it — it's going to be curtains for some of these smaller companies."

— CNBC'S Kif Leswing and Isabel O'Brien contributed reporting.

#2903. An early breakdown in the polar vortex is threatening already depleted gas stocks

An unprecedented early breakdown of the stratospheric polar vortex and a record-breaking El Niño event has put energy traders on alert this winter, with analysts warning the weather and climate phenomena could have an outsized impact on Europe.

Meteorologists are currently observing a record early breakdown of the polar vortex, a large-scale circulation of strong winds that typically keeps extremely cold air over the North Pole.

Changes in the strength and shape of the polar vortex can influence weather patterns much lower down in the atmosphere, potentially bringing in colder weather to parts of Europe and North America.

This year, however, the collapse of the stratospheric polar vortex, or SPV, is taking place at the same time as an unusually powerful El Niño, a climate event that can boost global temperatures and disrupt rainfall.

The collision of these two events raises the risk of a prolonged cold spell that could lift heating demand and force European countries to draw down already-low reserves faster. That would leave the continent more reliant on imported liquefied natural gas (LNG) at a time when supplies from the Middle East are disrupted.

To be sure, Europe's natural gas storage levels started the winter at their lowest level in more than a decade.

The International Energy Agency warned earlier this month that low European storage levels and limited storage options in several LNG-importing countries in Asia and South America could intensify the competition for cargoes and put upward pressure on prices during periods of high demand.

Simon Lee, a lecturer in atmospheric science at the U.K.'s University of St Andrews, said the combination of a sudden stratospheric warming (SSW) with an El Niño event is not necessarily unusual, but meteorologists had been expecting a polar vortex breakdown in February — not as early as November.

The early SPV disruption is an "extremely rare" event that could influence winter weather in northwest Europe and the eastern U.S., Lee said, increasing the chance of colder conditions although likely not to the extent that a polar vortex event caused parts of Niagara Falls to partially freeze in January 2014.

"Geopolitics and weather are the two key volatility drives for energy and now they seem to be happening at the same time — and that's always the biggest risk for markets," Florence Schmit, senior energy strategist at Rabobank, told CNBC.

For months, meteorologists expected an El Niño event would result in a milder winter in Europe and relatively muted heating demand, Schmit said, which was broadly seen as welcome news given ongoing concerns over the continent's tight regional supplies for gas and crude oil.

The prospect of a colder-than-expected winter due to an early breakdown of the polar vortex could be "incredibly difficult" for Europe to navigate, Schmit warned. She noted that the region's gas storages have seen "very, very strong withdrawals" over the last two weeks, "which is quite unusual for this time of year."

"If you continue to see the same storage withdrawals over the next couple of months, that means energy prices will spike again — and they won't spike because of geopolitics. This time it will be the weather driving that spike and that volatility," Schmit said.

European gas prices have soared to levels not seen since the 2022 energy crisis in recent weeks, notching 103.9 euros ($113.99) per megawatt hour in late October. European benchmark Dutch TTF futures for December delivery stood at around 87.28 euros on Friday afternoon.

"We have never seen an early breakdown of the polar vortex and a super el Nino in the same winter so we have no historical precedent on how this will impact global gas demand," David Lewis, principal research analyst of gas and liquified natural gas at Wood Mackenzie, told CNBC by email.

El Niño is commonly associated with milder winters, while a SSW event typically leads to colder temperatures and higher snowfall, Lewis said, noting this could materialize in December and January within Europe.

"So far, the start of the European winter heating season has been quite cold but more importantly has seen low wind speeds across Northern Europe," Lewis said.

"The drawdown in stocks in the first half of November has been amongst the highest on record. With inventories starting the winter at their lowest level on record, any further deterioration in temperatures or wind speeds will have an outsized impact on European balances and lead to higher prices across Europe," he added.

#2902. Stats Rundown: 3 numbers to know as the Mavericks defeat the Los Angeles Clippers, 110-100

The Dallas Mavericks (9-7) defeated the Los Angeles Clippers (4-11), 110-100, in a Saturday night matchup at the American Airlines Center. The win makes the Mavericks' second straight and follows their 116-94 blowout win over the Golden State Warriors. The Mavericks were able to overcome the Clippers down the stretch and move to 4-1 in clutch games this year.

PJ Washington, Dereck Lively, Tarik Biberovic, and Sergio de Larrea were out for the Mavericks. The Clippers were without Bradley Beal and Brandon Ingram.

Kyrie Irving and Cooper Flagg combined for 47 points in the Mavericks' win, but shot a combined 14-of-40 from the field. Brook Lopez led the Clippers with 21.

Here are three numbers to know from Saturday night's win.

Dallas finished with 42 bench points in the win over Los Angeles. In Washington's absence, the Mavericks needed someone to answer the call, and Max Christie did so, finishing with 13 points on 5-of-7 shooting from the field. Performances like his against the Clippers beg the question of whether he can fit in a future built around Flagg, and why there was a need to extend him last summer.

Cooper Flagg made three shots from behind the arc tonight for the second straight game. He finished with 22 points, six rebounds, and eight assists in the Mavericks' win. The Mavericks needed shooting to take this one home, and Flagg was able to contribute that tonight.

#2897. Retirees face a looming deadline for required withdrawals — missing it could trigger a tax penalty

Every year, millions of Americans must take required withdrawals from retirement accounts by Dec. 31.

Amid the busy holiday season, it's easy to miss the year-end deadline for required minimum distributions — a mistake that can trigger a hefty IRS penalty, experts said.

"One of the most common RMD mistakes is waiting until the last minute," said certified financial planner Alex Canellopoulos, director of wealth at Vista Capital Partners in Portland, Oregon.

Most retirees must start RMDs from pretax accounts at age 73. The withdrawal is based on the previous year's account balances on Dec. 31, your age and an IRS "life expectancy factor."

The first annual RMD deadline is April 1 of the year after you turn 73, and future withdrawals are due by Dec. 31. If you wait until April 1 for the first one, you'll need two RMDs that year.

RMDs also apply to certain inherited IRAs if the original IRA owner reached RMD age before their death.

In 2023, more than 14.8 million taxpayers age 70 or older had a traditional individual retirement account, according to the latest IRS data. The average year-end market value of the accounts was about $330,000.

Deferring RMDs until the last minute raises the likelihood of mistakes, such as overlooking certain retirement accounts or making withdrawal calculation errors, Canellopoulos said.

Some 7% of Vanguard investors at RMD age missed their yearly withdrawal in 2025, up slightly from 6.7% in 2024, according to a Vanguard analysis of about 400,000 RMD-age retirees with IRA balances published in December 2025.

Among those investors, the average missed RMD was $10,500 in 2025, Vanguard told CNBC.

Investors who don't take RMDs by the annual deadline may owe an excise tax to the IRS, worth up to 25% of the amount they should have withdrawn. For example, the excise tax could be up to $2,625 for a $10,500 missed RMD.

If investors miss a required withdrawal, they could reduce the penalty to 10% if they "timely corrected" the RMD within two years and file Form 5329, according to the IRS.

In some cases, the IRS could waive the penalty entirely due to "reasonable error" if investors take "reasonable steps" to fix the shortfall, according to the agency. Investors can file Form 5329 with a letter of explanation to request the waiver.

"If you don't ask, you're not going to get it," said certified public accountant Tim Steffen, director of advanced planning at financial services firm Baird. "I've never heard of anybody being denied the waiver."

Investors who give to charity may be skipping a specific donation strategy that can reduce or eliminate their annual RMDs, according to Lawrence Pon, a CFP and CPA based in Redwood City, California.

They can use a so-called qualified charitable distribution, or QCD, Pon said.

A QCD has a dual tax benefit: It doesn't count as taxable income and may also satisfy at least part of an investor's yearly RMD, Pon said.

The move requires a direct transfer from an investor's pretax IRA to an eligible nonprofit organization. They can donate up to $111,000 in 2026. While QCD eligibility begins at age 70½, transfers won't start counting toward RMDs until an investor reaches their RMD age.

Generally, taxpayers only get a tax break for large charitable donations if they itemize deductions on their tax returns. But QCDs still provide a tax benefit for charitable gifts when investors use the standard deduction.

QCDs "provide a real benefit if you are not itemizing [tax deductions]," said Pon.

While there's no tax deduction for QCDs, the transfer won't increase a taxpayer's adjusted gross income, or AGI. Raising AGI can cause other tax consequences, such as higher premiums for Medicare Part B and Part D.

#2895. Mavericks vs Clippers Recap: Late half runs seal the game for theDallas, 110-100

The Dallas Mavericks (9-7) defeated the Los Angeles Clippers at home on Saturday night, winning 110-100. Kyrie Irving was the high point man in Dallas, scoring 25 while grabbing six rebounds and four assists. Brook Lopez scored 21 in defeat for the Clippers.

The opening period felt like a rock fight, with neither team scoring well in the early going. The Clippers scored a flurry of points in the final minutes to take an early lead. The Mavericks played poorly into the second and it looked like another one of those games. After falling behind 33-24, the Mavericks came to life in a big way. Walter Clayton Jr. and Max Christie led the charge and Dallas scored 13 straight. They held court the rest of the quarter and took a 52-44 lead into the half.

The Clippers wouldn't go away though, and Dallas spent the second half trading baskets with Los Angeles. Though Dallas remained in control, the game felt up in the air right up until the 4:18 mark. With Dallas ahead 91-88, a Daniel Gafford dunk was followed by a Max Christie steal and dunk to give Dallas some cushion. Over the next minute, the lead grew to 14 before the Clippers would finally answer but by that time the damage was done. Dallas closed out the game with the final score 110-100.

The margin of this game also happens to be the same margin of the two teams' fast-break points: The Clippers had 12 and the Mavericks had 22. Coach Dusty May has mentioned since preseason that playing fast would be key for Dallas in wins and tonight it proved decisive.

It starts with defense, of course, and Dallas played with spots of frenetic activity that one can point to in what was a closer game than I expected.

It's safe to say that Max Christie has had a disappointing season, barring a few exceptions. Tonight against the Clippers is one and his 13 points and 24 minutes were absolutely needed. It's only the second double-digit minute performance for Christie this month as he's been dealing with injuries and poor play.

The Mavericks don't need Christie to be a star by any means, but they do need playable performances. He's got to have the confidence to take and make shots when presented with them and hopefully his play tonight will lead to more confidence in the coming weeks.

Past the fastbreak points, that the Mavericks were able to not turn the ball over contributed directly to the win. The Clippers committed 16 turnovers and it lead to 22 Dallas points. The Mavericks handed it over to Los Angeles just nine times and ceded 13 points in comparison.

The funny part is this season Dallas has about the same amount of turnoers in wins as they do in losses. They average about 15 per contest so most games it doesn't really make a difference. Tonight though, you can see it directly. They controlled the ball better and that small margin made the difference.

#2892. Nvidia stock plunges 14%, wraps up longest weekly losing streak since 2008 financial crisis

The last time Wall Street was this down on Nvidia the chipmaker's flagship product was the GeForce graphics processor for high-end PC video games, and markets were reeling from the global financial crisis.

Nvidia shares tumbled 14% on Friday, sliding for a seventh straight day as investors grew increasingly concerned about life after co-founder Jensen Huang. The stock closed lower for a seventh consecutive week, the longest such stretch since 2008.

Shares of Nvidia are down 46% over the period, pushing the company's market cap down by about $2.6 trillion. The stock is now trading at roughly 8.4 times forward earnings, while Advanced Micro Devices is valued at 29 times profit for the next 12 months.

On Friday, Reuters reported that the Securities and Exchange Commission is exploring potential accounting issues at Nvidia, pushing the stock down further.

Once the most valuable company in the world, Nvidia now has a market cap of $3 trillion, putting it fourth behind Apple, Microsoft and Alphabet.

Nvidia is reeling from the removal of Huang, who resigned on Nov. 12, after multiple reported affairs, including one with a Chinese businesswoman who was allegedly a spy.

Harvey Jones, Nvidia's newly appointed board chair, said earlier this week that Huang had resigned after "disclosure of personal behavior inconsistent with the company's code of conduct, standards and cultural norms."

But the chipmaker's losing streak started before Nvidia was thrown into crisis as investors worried over the sustainability of the AI buildout, particularly after the midterm elections, and emerging competition in AI chips from Google and AMD.

Even a better-than-expected earnings report, which included an increase to fiscal 2028 guidance, didn't stem the selling. Interim CEO Colette Kress, the company's longtime CFO, projected revenue growth next fiscal year of 75%, and said that customers aren't going anywhere.

On Tuesday's earnings call with analysts, Kress signed off by saying, "we've got this." In a company meeting the next day, executives told staff that it was moving its yearly review process, which can come with more stock, from March to December.

For investors, one major question is whether Nvidia can continue to innovate and sustain its commanding lead in the AI chip market without its visionary leader. Almost the entire company now is built around the AI trade, a phenomenon that began taking hold just four years ago with the launch of OpenAI's ChatGPT.

Before that, Nvidia's primary business was selling graphics cards used for video games. In mid-2008, the last time the stock was this deep into a weekly losing streak, Nvidia was generating less than $1 billion per quarter. Now it pulls in more than $1 billion per day. At the time, the entire market was in freefall due to the worst financial crisis since the Great Depression.

#2891. Player Grades: Recapping the Mavericks’ 110-100 win over the Los Angeles Clippers

The Dallas Mavericks (9-7) won 110-100 against the Los Angeles Clippers (4-11) on Saturday in the first game of a back-to-back set that finds them traveling to Utah on Sunday. With the quick turnaround and three games in four nights, getting a win against the struggling Clippers was crucial. Now Dallas takes a two-game winning streak on the road.

Irving struggled on a high volume of shots, connecting on only 8-of-24 overall and only 3-of-11 from downtown. Irving is still Irving, however, and that means a powerful fourth quarter performance can be on tap for any game. Saturday night, he poured in nearly half his points (12) in the fourth quarter alone to boost his grade.

Flagg got into foul trouble with two quick ones in the opening minutes. That may have impacted his flow, but his overall performance wasn't the smoothest. He committed four fouls and had three turnovers on a poor shooting night. He was 6-of-16 overall and only 7-of-13 from the free throw line. He was the worst free throw offender on the team while shooting six more free throws than the next closest teammate.

Martin remains almost impossible to grade. He rarely contributes to all five primary statistical categories, and tonight was no different. He does all the little things that don't show up on the books, but his 0-for-3 shooting pulls his grade down in this one.

Risacher was close to awesome in under 30 minutes. He numbers don't necessarily scream "awesome," however he did just about everything right. 5-of-11 shooting, 1-of-2 from beyond the arc, no turnovers and a dive on the floor that almost cost him his nose, but showed a terrific level of commitment to effort in getting the win.

The eye test would tell you an "8" is a bit too high, but the actual output justifies it. On the heels of a nice win, Gafford gets the benefit of the doubt. He hit 4-of-5 from the floor, was a single rebound shy of a double-double and had nice assist and steal totals. He had a ridiculous plus-22 plus/minus to lead all players.

Clayton had a huge game relative to his limited minutes. His hot shooting saw him go 5-of-8 from the floor and 2-of-3 from downtown. One of those threes came in the fourth quarter at a huge moment to put Dallas up by five points.

Christie looked like his best self for just about the first time all season. He nailed 5-of-7 overall and 3-of-4 from deep. This is exactly what the Mavs need from him, especially on a night where P.J. Washington's potential three point shooting was on the bench. He also grabbed three steals, demonstrating the better side of his defense.

Johnson's grade is boosted by doing what he did in less than a quarter-and-a-half. 16 games into his career, this level of output for a full game would be nice to see. To maximize his limited time this way was impressive. Johnson, Jr. shot 4-of-9 including connecting on his only three-point attempt.

This felt like a must-win. Dallas struggled mightily on the road recently and has been doing well at home. Dropping one to a four-win team before heading out for three on the road would have been… not good. The good news is, Mavs Nation doesn't have to worry about that, as Dallas took care of business with a big fourth quarter to close out the Clippers.

Take your pick in Chargers-Jets, since the Bills have both teams on this season's schedule. Assuredly, you're fans of the Giants and Raiders this weekend.

#2886. Down Tatum, White, and Robinson, Celtics lose to lowly Kings 97-96

The Celtics suffered their most embarrassing loss of the season on Saturday night, losing to the now two-win Sacramento Kings, 97-96.

It was the third quarter that pushed the Kings over the top as Boston shot 5-of-26 from the field (missed all thirteen three-pointers) in the period and were outscored 35-13. It was a great game for Mike Conley, who scored 19 points and was a +17.

The Celtics were missing three key players in this one; Jayson Tatum, Derrick White and Mitchell Robinson all sat out this game. They started Payton Pritchard, Sam Hauser, Paul George, Hugo Gonzalez and Neemias Queta. It was the first start of the season for both Hauser and Pritchard. Meanwhile, the Kings started, Darius Acuff Jr., Zach LaVine, Keegan Murray, De'Andre Hunter and Domantas Sabonis. Ben Simmons and Maxime Raynaud remained out as neither player has played all season.

It was a really hot start for the Celtics as they scored the first 7 points of the game — buckets from Pritchard, Queta and George, led to a Sacramento timeout.

The C's missed 11 of their first 13 three point attempts as Sacramento went on a 21-5 run to take a 23-19 lead.

Sam Hauser hit a corner three at the buzzer to give the Celtics a 27-25 lead at the end of the first quarter. Hauser led the team in scoring with 6 points while Hugo Gonzalez had 5.

Mike Conley was the first point guard off of the bench instead of Devin Carter in what was his return to Sacramento. Conley chipped in right away with 5 points and 2 steals in his first 4 minutes of action.

He then had a sweet pass to Ron Harper Jr.

The Celtics took a lot of threes in the first half but most of them did not go in. They went 9/33 from beyond the arc in the half. They also fouled too much in the first half; the Kings spent much of the 2nd quarter in the bonus.

The Celtics led 54-41 after the first half. Hauser had 4 of the 9 Celtics threes and 12 points. The defense was awesome holding the Kings to 41 points and being the reason Boston led by 13 at the break.

The Celtics three-point struggles continued into the 3rd quarter as the Kings made a run to cut the Celtics lead to 2 at 60-58. The primary reason for the Kings run was the Celtics defense fell off. The Kings scored 17 points in the first 6:31 of the 3rd quarter. The Celtics called a timeout and turned it over out of the timeout as Sacramento tied the game. The Kings led entering the 4th, 76-67.

There are really no words to describe how the quarter went. They were bad on offense and defense and this is against the worst 3rd quarter team in the NBA entering the night — the Kings 3rd quarter net rating had been -23.7.

The Celtics started the 4th quarter with a new lineup.

They scored the first 1o points of the quarter to take the lead by 1. Mike Conley had 6 of the 10 points.

The two teams were tied at 82 after the Kings regained a 5-point lead but another Conley three and a Luka Garza put back knotted the game up.

Then, on a night the Celtics could not buy a three, Neemias Queta hit a three.

Darius Acuff hit a tough mid-range shot to put the Kings up 1, followed by a Paul George offensive rebound to put the Celtics up 1, then Acuff made 2 free throws to put the Kings up 1 with 40.5 seconds to go.

After Queta and Acuff traded 2 free throws each, Payton Pritchard, who had struggled all night, hit a clutch mid-range shot to put the Celtics up 1 with 11.8 seconds to go as the Kings called timeout.

Keegan Murray got an offensive rebound and put back right over Devin Carter and Hugo Gonzalez to put the Kings up 97-96 with 5 seconds left.

Payton Pritchard missed what would have been a game winning shot and the Celtics lost 97-96 as the Kings got their 2nd win of the season.

In a game Boston lost by 1 points, they missed 10 free throws.

For what it's worth, Darius Acuff Jr. is a star. The way he took over the game late after struggling all night was really impressive.

The C's shot 34% from the field and 22% from three while the Kings shot 41% from the field and 27% from three. The Celtics next game is Sunday in San Francisco against the Warriors at 8:30 pm EST.

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2884. How China is using summits to expand its global influence

SHENZHEN, China — For a second year in a row, Chinese President Xi Jinping has brought Russian President Vladimir Putin together with another major world leader on mainland Chinese shores.

U.S. President Donald Trump had a trilateral meeting with Xi and Putin on Thursday, after the three met the prior evening during a welcome banquet for the Asia-Pacific Economic Leaders' Meeting in Shenzhen. It marked the first time they had met together in person.

In September 2025, Xi, Putin and Indian Prime Minister Narendra Modi briefly laughed together for a friendly photo-op alongside an annual meeting of the Shanghai Cooperation Organization in Tianjin, China. The gathering followed easing tensions between Beijing and New Delhi.

China's "ability to engage directly with the world major powers" reflects the country's influence across the Global South and Eurasia, said Jodie Wen, postdoctoral fellow at the Center for International Security and Strategy at Tsinghua University. "China hopes to play a more constructive role in this conflict-[ridden], unstable world."

In the future, "China will hope to provide more platforms" within its borders for countries to discuss issues, she said.

Trump, Xi, Putin said they plan to boost oil production, revamp UN after trilateral talks

I watched Trump, Xi and Putin gather in China. Here are my top takeaways

Trump holds talks separately with Putin and Xi ahead of their historic trilateral meeting

Takaichi gets chilly reception in China on first Japan leader visit since 2019

Why the Middle East is watching the Trump-Xi-Putin summit

Trump arrives in China for historic meeting with Xi, Putin

China's Xi calls for removing trade and investment barriers

Putin says EU is losing industry, falling behind competition

Three things to watch as Trump, Xi and Putin meet in China

China has increasingly played the role of broker, for instance, providing the forum for restoring diplomatic ties between Saudi Arabia and Iran in 2023.

By participating in and hosting different multilateral organizations, China has stayed in the Western orbit while cultivating relations with non-Western countries. The two photo-ops last year and this year reflect only a portion of Xi's large number of meetings with different world leaders.

Xi has hosted more than 40 heads of state so far this year, including at least eight around the APEC meetings in Shenzhen this week. That's on top of receiving over 60 state visits in 2025, according to CNBC analysis of official Chinese readouts.

Domestic Chinese media have highlighted how Xi has met with many foreign leaders around the China-hosted events.

"Beijing sees these multilateral platforms as opportunities to expand its international influence and demonstrate its commitment to multilateralism, while bilateral diplomacy remains equally important," said Yue Su, principal economist for China at EIU.

But she noted that different countries increasingly pursue different geopolitical and economic priorities, making it challenging to reach consensus even through multilateral organizations.

"Against this backdrop, China's diplomatic priorities are increasingly focused on delivering tangible economic benefits through trade, investment, financial connectivity and technology cooperation," she said.

On Thursday, Xi announced at the APEC Economic Leaders' Meeting that China would provide $20 million in support for the Asia Pacific region. He also advocated for the development of an "Asia-Pacific community with a shared future," an iteration of his commonly used slogan on a "shared future" for humanity more broadly.

The Shenzhen summit can create meaningful outcomes for crisis management, as well as diplomatic space for future negotiations, Tsinghua University's Wen said. She added that it's important for leaders to come together in person in order to avoid misunderstandings and further tensions.

Trump said Thursday at the trilateral that the U.S., China and Russia are "uniquely positioned" to discuss reducing nuclear weapons at their historic meeting. A Chinese readout subsequently said the three leaders discussed Ukraine, the Middle East and the Korean Peninsula.

—CNBC's Matthew Tan contributed to this report.

#2883. Travis Kelce scores twice, passes Rob Gronkowski on all-time tight end touchdown list in blowout win over Cardinals

Travis Kelce finally jumped Rob Gronkowski on Sunday afternoon.

Kelce hauled in an 8-yard touchdown early in the Kansas City Chiefs' 34-7 blowout win over the Arizona Cardinals on Sunday. That marked the 109th score of Kelce's career, which moved him to third on the NFL's all-time touchdown list by a tight end and broke a tie with Gronkowski. The longtime New England Patriots star finished his career with 108 touchdowns.

Now, only Hall of Famers Antonio Gates, with 118, and Tony Gonzalez, with 115, sit ahead of Kelce.

Kelce scored again in the second quarter when Patrick Mahomes found him for a 22-yard touchdown. Kelce jumped up and made the grab between a trio of Cardinals.

That touchdown put the Chiefs up 23-7 at the time.

Kelce finished the game with 74 receiving yards on six receptions in the 27-point win, which pushed the Chiefs to 8-2 on the season. Mahomes went 27-of-39 passing for 324 yards with three touchdowns. He sat early in the fourth quarter with the win all but secured. The Cardinals (3-8) have lost three straight.

Sunday was also Kelce's 100th regular-season game at Arrowhead Stadium, which made him the fourth player in team history to reach that mark. Kelce has already broken the NFL record for most yards after catch in league history this season, too, and earlier this fall Mahomes and Kelce surpassed Gates and Philip Rivers for the most yards generated by a tight end and quarterback duo in NFL history.

Kelce has spent his entire career with the Chiefs, who first selected him out of Cincinnati in the third round of the 2013 NFL Draft. He has 13,588 receiving yards in his career, which trails only Gonzalez among tight ends, and he has 586 receiving yards and four touchdowns this season.

While Kelce's career is winding down, he is only nine touchdowns shy of becoming the all-time tight end touchdown leader. That may be tough to pull off with just seven games left in the regular season, but the mark is in reach.

#2881. Mike Brown pushing for faster offense from sluggish Knicks

"Last-last," Brown asked, "or second-to-last?"

Entering Saturday, the Knicks sat at the very bottom of the NBA in pace, averaging 94.91 possessions per game. Brown repeatedly has urged his team to play with pace since taking over last year, but it's a rare desire that has not come to fruition.

New York Knicks head coach Mike Brown reacts during the fourth quarter against the Portland Trail Blazers in New York, Saturday, Nov. 21, 2026. Getty Images

The reality is that this is not an overly athletic roster that thrives in transition. This group of veteran players never will be a unit that loves to get up and down the floor. Jalen Brunson, Karl-Anthony Towns and OG Anunoby are more methodical scorers who prefer to set up themselves in the frontcourt.

Brown knows that, so while he will continue to preach pace, he values something else that serves as an indication of whether his offense is functioning properly, something that doesn't necessarily require the natural athleticism they lack.

"We have to make sure that every time we cut, it's a scoring cut and our decisions are quick," Brown said before a 97-96 Knicks win, which required a buzzer-beating 3 from Mikal Bridges to escape after blowing a 26-point second-half lead.

When the Knicks offense kicked into gear during their championship run last year, it was evident.

With Towns operating as a hub, the four other Knicks on the floor constantly cut and set off-ball screens around him. The ball moved quickly, swinging side to side constantly. Towns, along with Anunoby and Jordan Clarkson, did not play Saturday.

Landry Shamet, who scored 20 points, shoots a jumper over Toumani Camara during the Knicks' buzzer-beating win over the Trail Blazers on Nov. 21, 2026, at the Garden. Jason Szenes for New York Post

"You can tell when a guy goes from Point A to Point B with a little bit of velocity," Brown said. "A guy like Landry Shamet, he is doing it every single time. With him shooting the ball the way he's shooting it, that gravity that he carries when he moves, you can't really see it or feel it if you don't know the game well. But when he moves that quickly from Point A to Point B, everybody is just leaning just a little bit in that direction, which may create an opening or an advantage for your teammate that's away from him."

It's a reason why Shamet's role keeps growing. Among the five starters, though, it's something they've had to reestablish after a sluggish first few games of the season.

The Knicks aren't quick in transition. Once they're set in the frontcourt, though, they're capable of playing quickly.

It's when they are at their best. It takes pressure off Brunson having to orchestrate everything. It sets up open catch-and-shoot opportunities for Anunoby and Mikal Bridges. It allows Josh Hart to utilize his passing ability and set up teammates.

"Trying to get everybody to do that all the time is huge," Brown said. "Because the defense doesn't have a chance to catch up, they don't have a chance to get set, and they just gotta react. And sometimes they'll overreact because you're going so hard. And you can see it as the game goes along. If guys aren't doing it, we try to mention it to him."

A slow team finding a way to play fast — that's crucial to the Knicks' success.

#2880. I never wanted the American Dream and I felt like something was wrong with me—until I moved abroad

I got my first job at 17, working the register at an Eckerd's drugstore in Texas. My manager was only a few years older than me, and while my coworkers seemed motivated by the idea of working their way up to his position, I felt no desire to follow that path.

It all felt suffocating: the added responsibility, the longer hours, the idea that this was the ladder I was supposed to want to climb. I wanted to work, but I couldn't wrap my head around building my whole identity around a job.

I never wanted the American Dream I saw so many people around me striving to achieve. For a long time, I wondered if something was wrong with me. It wasn't until I finally made a long-term move abroad about a decade ago that I realized it was okay to want something different.

After college, I got a job in travel sales. I was good at it. Talking about travel all day suited the wanderlust I'd carried since my first trip abroad in my teens.

I continued working in sales, marketing and the odd temp role for years. But they were always just jobs. I didn't feel like they defined me or feel a calling to an industry or title the way people around me seemed to.

Of course, I wanted financial stability, but I was unwilling to sacrifice my time and well-being to get it. I just didn't connect with the version of ambition that meant grinding toward a corner office. What I wanted the most wasn't status; it was freedom, growth and new experiences.

The wanderlust eventually caught up to me, and at 26 I moved to Spain to teach English. Even though I was working, people saw it as taking a year off from my career and warned me it could damage my resume. I simply didn't care. Living for my resume had never made sense to me.

Looking back, I think being abroad offered an escape from the pressure to chase the traditional path of the American Dream. There, I felt free to enjoy life and figure out who I was without worrying about what box I needed to tick next.

I wanted to stay in Spain, but I couldn't find a visa that would let me live there long-term, so I returned to the U.S. I became a serial job hopper for almost 10 years, quitting a job every year to spend summers visiting friends in the Netherlands, the U.K. and Switzerland or solo traveling in Spain on the money I'd saved up.

On the personal side, I wasn't in a rush to get married or have kids. In my 30s, single and childfree, I felt like I was failing at adulthood, even though deep down, I didn't actually want the things I was supposedly behind on. Settling down just because everyone else was doing it never felt right to me.

And I couldn't shake the dream of living in Europe. In my 20s, people mostly assumed my desire to travel was a phase. By my 30s, the conversations had shifted and I was getting pulled aside. Friends, family and colleagues all had some version of the same message: It was time to get serious and build a life in Texas.

But my gut said to try one more time. So I did.

In 2015, on my fourth attempt, I finally made it to Spain for good. It was scary to shake things up when everyone around me was settling down, but deep down I knew at 35, this was my chance to stop trying to conform and start building a life that felt right for me.

In my new home, the pace was slower. I quickly noticed that people actually took their vacations. Strangers at a dinner party didn't immediately ask me what I did for work. Adults in their 30s, 40s and beyond were out living full social lives. Single, childfree women my age didn't feel like an anomaly.

Living abroad didn't solve everything overnight. I still had to figure out how to support myself. But having the space to define myself professionally without constant judgment changed what felt possible.

In 2017, I launched She Hit Refresh, where I help women over 30 figure out how to move to Europe and build new lives for themselves. It's not something I could have created from anywhere else, with any other experience than my own.

In the process, I've met so many others who left corporate America behind to create something different for themselves. I've seen more women asking the same questions I spent years asking myself. Burnt out, disillusioned with hustle culture, realizing that checking all the boxes of the American Dream they grew up on didn't deliver the feeling they were promised.

We're all figuring it out together; each of us writing a version of a life that didn't fit a standard template. Moving abroad helped me understand that success, for me, was never about earning more or owning more. It was always about more ease, more time, more space, more life.

For years, I thought I was behind. I felt like something was wrong with me for wanting a different kind of life. Looking back, I just hadn't found my place yet.

Cepee Tabibian is the founder of She Hit Refresh, where she's helped over 24,000 women age 30+ plan their move abroad. Grab her free Move Abroad After 30 Guide to start planning your own move. The daughter of Colombian and Iranian immigrants, Cepee grew up in Houston, Texas, before becoming an immigrant herself in Spain. Follow her @shehitrefresh.
